I’ve put in requests, but you know how budgets are.\n\nBut… the part that scared me is I’ll hear it even when every channel is muted. Like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And when I ask someone else, they don’t hear anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear the voices when you are at home or in other places away from work?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly at work. In that surveillance room, during the quiet stretches, with all the monitors and those old speakers. I know I keep saying the speakers are old, but they crackle and pop anyway, so at first I blamed that. We’ve needed new equipment forever.\n\nBut then every channel will be muted, completely muted, and I’ll still hear it—“You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” That’s what really scared me, because I’ll ask someone else and they don’t hear anything. At home… I don’t think it’s like that. It’s really that room, those monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voices only through the speakers or can you hear them in your mind as well?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s what makes it so hard to explain, because I’m sitting there in the surveillance room and it’s quiet, and those old monitor speakers are right there—honestly, they crackle even on a normal day, and I’ve told them maintenance should look at it.\n\nBut then every channel will be muted, and I still hear it. Like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears it. I don’t… I don’t really think of it as in my mind exactly. It’s like it’s coming from the equipment, even when it shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard those voices when you were not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, what scared me enough to come in was that it happens in the surveillance room, during the quiet stretches. I’ll be sitting there with the monitors, and it sounds like it’s coming through the speakers, saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd the thing is, every channel can be muted. I’ll check it, because that’s my job, I know the system, and still… I hear it. Then nobody else hears anything when I ask. So no, I don’t think I’ve heard it away from work like that. It’s that room.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever with someone else when you hear the voices through the speakers?\n\nPatient: Sometimes there are other people around, yes. Not always right next to me, because in that room people come and go, and most of the time it’s just me watching the monitors. But when it happens, I’ll ask, “Did you hear that?” and they look at me like… no, like nothing came through.\n\nThat’s part of what scared me. It sounds like it’s coming through the speakers, saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and I’ll check the channels and they’re muted. And then I start thinking about how we’ve complained about the equipment for ages, the crackling and all that, but this isn’t just crackling. Nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see anything unusual when you hear the voices through the speakers?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I see anything, no—not like a person standing there or something moving on the screens. It’s more the sound that gets me.\n\nI’ll be in that surveillance room during the quiet part of the shift, and the speakers are right there, and I start checking because that’s what you do—you don’t just ignore equipment acting up. We’ve had those monitors forever, and half the time something is buzzing or crackling, and management acts like you’re being dramatic when you mention it.\n\nBut this is different. The channels can all be muted, and I still hear it saying, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And then I look around, check the screens, ask if anyone else heard it, and they didn’t. So… no, not really seeing things. It’s the voices through the speakers that scare me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voices through the speakers when you're feeling calm and relaxed at work?\n\nPatient: Calm and relaxed… I don’t know that I’m ever exactly relaxed in that room. It’s quiet, but it’s not peaceful, if that makes sense. You’re just staring at all those monitors, and the longer it stays quiet, the more you start thinking you must be missing something.\n\nThat’s usually when it happens—the quiet stretches. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers, like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And I’ll check, because the speakers, the channels, all of it can be muted. I know how the system works. But even muted, I still hear it, and nobody else hears anything when I ask.\n\nSo… not when I feel calm, exactly. More when it’s quiet in there.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually alone when you hear the voices through the speakers?\n\nPatient: Usually, yes… I mean, not always in the whole department, but in that surveillance room it’s often just me watching the monitors, especially during the quiet stretches. That’s when it scares me the most.\n\nI’ll hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers—“You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you”—and I start checking everything because, you know, those speakers have been bad for ages and everyone complains about the equipment. But then every channel is muted, and still I hear it.\n\nIf someone is nearby, I’ll ask them, and they don’t hear anything. So… it feels like I’m alone with it, even if there are people somewhere around.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear through the speakers always accusing or critical of you?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can’t think of them saying anything kind or normal, like a regular announcement or something.\n\nIt’s always that kind of thing through the monitor speakers—You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And that’s what scared me enough to come in, because I check the channels, I mute everything, I do what I’m supposed to do, and it still sounds like it’s coming through there. Then nobody else hears it.\n\nSo… yes, it’s accusing. It feels like it’s blaming me.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear through the speakers always saying the same things?\n\nPatient: Not exactly the same every time, no… but it’s the same kind of thing, if that makes sense. Like it circles around the same blame.\n\nIt’ll be something like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. Sometimes I can’t remember the exact wording after, because then I’m busy checking the channels and the speakers and everything. Those monitors make little sounds anyway, and the equipment’s old, so at first I try to tell myself it’s that.\n\nBut what scared me is I’ll mute every channel and still hear it, like it’s coming through the speakers. And when I ask somebody else, they don’t hear anything. So the words aren’t always identical, but it’s always that same accusing feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voices through the speakers when you're not under any work-related stress or pressure?\n\nPatient: It’s not always when it’s busy, no. Actually… that’s part of what makes it worse, I think. It happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m just watching the monitors and there isn’t some big incident going on.\n\nThe speakers in there have always been a problem—everyone says that, not just me—so at first I think it’s the equipment. But then I’ll hear, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you, and I’ll check the channels, mute everything, go through it all… and it’s still there. And if I ask someone else, they don’t hear it.\n\nSo, no, it doesn’t have to be when I’m under pressure. It can happen when it’s quiet, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear the voices when you are not at work and not near any kind of monitor or speaker?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like that.\n\nIt’s mostly in the surveillance room, during those quiet stretches when I’m sitting there watching the monitors. That room has these old speakers, and everybody knows they make little noises, so I’ll start thinking, well, maybe it’s just the equipment again. But then it sounds like a voice saying, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you.\n\nAnd I’ll mute everything, every channel, and it still seems like it’s coming through there. That’s the part that scared me. If it was just at home or walking around somewhere, I’d maybe… I don’t know. But no one else hears it when I ask. So, no, not away from work and speakers that I can remember. It’s been there, through the monitor speakers.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever say anything that isn't about something you've supposedly done wrong or missed?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’m trying to think if there was ever something different, like just a random word or somebody talking about the weather or something, but no. It’s always tied to me having missed something.\n\nIt’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And then I start going through the channels again, because the speakers in that room make noises anyway—everyone knows they’re old, they crackle and hum—so I keep thinking maybe I’m just catching something from another feed. I used to work with this one monitor that would buzz whenever the vending machine outside kicked on, so I know equipment can do strange things.\n\nBut this is different. Even when I mute every channel, it still sounds like it’s coming through the speakers. And when I ask someone else, they don’t hear anything. So… no, I don’t think it says other kinds of things. It’s mostly that accusing stuff, like I did something wrong or missed something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are coming from inside your head or truly from the speakers?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s truly from the speakers. That’s what scares me.\n\nI know that sounds… I don’t know how it sounds, but in that surveillance room, during the quiet parts, it’s like it’s coming right through the monitor speakers. And those speakers are old, they do make noises—there’s always some hum or crackle, and one of the guys used to joke that they were haunted, which I didn’t think was funny even then, but anyway—\n\nI’ll hear, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And I’ll mute every channel, check everything, and it still seems like it’s coming from there. Not like a thought inside my head. More like… like the room is saying it through the equipment. But when I ask someone else, they don’t hear anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices would stop if the surveillance room's speakers were replaced with new ones?\n\nPatient: I want to think that, yes. I keep telling myself if they just put in new speakers, maybe it would stop, because the ones in there are old and they crackle and hum all the time.\n\nBut… I don’t know. The part that made me come in is that I’ve heard it even after I muted every channel. It still sounded like it was coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And no one else hears it when I ask.\n\nSo I’d hope new speakers would fix it, but I’m scared it wouldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices would continue if you were in a completely silent room with no speakers or external noise?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nI want to say no, because if there were no speakers, then how could it be coming through speakers, right? But the thing that scared me enough to come in is that in the surveillance room, when it’s quiet, I still hear it through the monitor speakers even when I’ve muted every channel. Like, not just a crackle—words. You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you.\n\nAnd I’ll ask someone else, and they don’t hear anything. So if I were in a totally silent room with no speakers… I don’t know if it would keep going. I’m afraid it might.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices would still be there if you were in the surveillance room but wearing headphones that completely blocked out all external sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I keep trying to picture that, like if I had the big noise-canceling headphones on—my brother has a pair for mowing the lawn, actually, he swears by them, though I don’t know if those are the same kind you mean—\n\nBut the thing is, what scared me enough to come here was that in the surveillance room, during the quiet stretches, I hear the voices through the monitor speakers. Even when every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you, and I’ll check and check, and nobody else hears anything.\n\nSo if headphones blocked everything out… I want to say maybe that would prove it’s the speakers, but I’m scared I’d still somehow hear it, or feel like it was still coming from there. I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: If you were in a completely silent room with no speakers or external noise, would you still hear the voices?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I keep trying to make it fit with the room, because that’s where it happens—the surveillance room, when it gets quiet, like late in a shift when there’s just that hum and the screens going. And I know those monitor speakers are old; one of them has this buzz that drives me crazy, and I’ve asked maintenance before, but maintenance never comes when you actually need them, you know?\n\nBut… what scared me enough to come in is that I hear it even when every channel is muted. It still sounds like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And when I ask someone else, they don’t hear anything.\n\nSo in a silent room with no speakers… I don’t know. I want to say no, but I’m afraid I might still hear it somehow.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I keep trying to picture that, like if I had the big noise-canceling headphones on—my brother has a pair for mowing the lawn, actually, he swears by them, though I don’t know if those are the same kind you mean—\n\nBut the thing is, what scared me enough to come here was that in the surveillance room, during the quiet stretches, I hear the voices through the monitor speakers. Even when every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you, and I’ll check and check, and nobody else hears anything.\n\nSo if headphones blocked everything out… I want to say maybe that would prove it’s the speakers, but I’m scared I’d still somehow hear it, or feel like it was still coming from there. I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: If you were in a completely silent room with no speakers or external noise, would you still hear the voices?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I keep trying to make it fit with the room, because that’s where it happens—the surveillance room, when it gets quiet, like late in a shift when there’s just that hum and the screens going. And I know those monitor speakers are old; one of them has this buzz that drives me crazy, and I’ve asked maintenance before, but maintenance never comes when you actually need them, you know?\n\nBut… what scared me enough to come in is that I hear it even when every channel is muted. It still sounds like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. And when I ask someone else, they don’t hear anything.\n\nSo in a silent room with no speakers… I don’t know. I want to say no, but I’m afraid I might still hear it somehow."
  }
]
